Report by the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the draft law amending the Law on the Prosecutor's Office and other laws, print No 2731. At its 67th meeting on 19 July, the Sejm sent the above draft to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights for consideration. At the same time, the Sejm set a deadline for submitting the draft report by 19 July. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights, after considering this proposal at its meeting on 19 July, asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. The meeting ended at 3 p.m. 1 at night, but the practice of this House is that in such a situation we assume that the sitting took place on 19 July, and this is no exception to the principle that has been applied in this House for many years. The Commission shall act in accordance with Article. Point 8 (4) gives a new wording to this point. 28. minority application: in Article. 8 (5) amend the following wording: The requirement to have only Polish citizenship does not apply to judges, assesors, appointed to perform office before the date of entry into force of this Act. 29. minority application: delete Article. 20. 30.: delete Article. 21. 31.: delete Article. 22. 32.: add a new wording to the article. 24 on the entry into force of the Act: 3 months after the date of publication. As regards the detailed presentation of the proposed law, the law is amended as follows: the Law amending the Law on the system of common courts and certain other laws. And so, Mr. Marshal, in the scope of amending the Law on the Law on the System of Universal Courts, there are several changes, among others. amendments to remove the doubts raised by the courts concerning the powers of court assessors to rule on family law cases. The next amendment aims to entrust the head of the department of perpetual books, the economic departments for register matters and the economic departments for KRS cases only to judicial referenda. These changes allow for more flexible administration of courts by derogating from the principle that each district court must divide into civil and criminal departments. The proposed amendments provide the possibility for colleges of courts to vote not only during meetings but also on a circular basis. The amendment aims to eliminate quorum at meetings of the General Assembly of Judges. Consequence of repeal in point 2 of Article. 34 U.S.P., i.e. the General Court Act, i.e. the introduction of the Law of 12 July on the system of courts to amend the model of appointment of the Presidents of the courts. Repeal in Article 41 is the result of the adoption that the departments in charge of the division of cases into categories in which cases are allocated randomly should be governed by the order of the Minister of Justice. The amendment then shifts the application of the principle of non-change of composition only to the stage after the first meeting. A reduction is introduced in the number of signatures of the files in which documents should be submitted to those who wish to apply for the vacant posts of judges. And a few more comments on the revision of the D.A.'s law. The purpose of this amendment is: to change the nature of the prosecutor's presence as a participant in the proceedings, to make the terminology changes, to credit the period of the previous service. These changes are before the High House, the committee's report has been delivered to everyone. At the same time, as rapporteur for the committee, I must refer to the proceedings of this committee. Ladies and gentlemen, the committee's meeting has been interrupted all the time by Members of the opposition who have been aiming to ensure that the meeting cannot be held or that it will last for a very long time. Not so big a change - because there are many more - has led to the meeting being held for 10 hours. Even the independent Member who was present was outraged by the opposition Members' way of acting, who were only aiming at one thing: destabilizing the committee meeting and getting the chairman of the committee off balance. Unfortunately, they failed because the committee properly conducted the procedure.

On the night of 20 to 21 August 1968, troops of the Soviet Union, Bulgaria, GDR, Poland and Hungary entered Czechoslovakia. It was an armed intervention of the Warsaw Pact, which was the Kremlin's response to economic and social reforms initiated by Alexander Dubček. Over 100 people were killed as a result of the invasion and more than 800 were injured. These events forced changes in the leadership of the Czechoslovakian authorities and a retreat from all reforms. The new authorities began repression of people involved in the 'Prussian Spring'. It's emigrated from the country. then about 300,000 people. The response to the invasion was dramatic and tragic protests. They burned themselves: Jan Palach, Jan Zajic and Evzen Ploček. In Poland, the symbol of protest against the participation of the army of the Polish People's Republic in the invasion was the death of Richard Siwc, a former soldier of the National Army who set himself on fire during the Harvest Festival at the Ten Years Stadium in Warsaw, earlier developing a white and red flag with the inscription: 'for our freedom and your freedom'. On the 50th anniversary of the Invasion of the troops of the Warsaw Pact for Czechoslovakia, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to all its victims and those who, resisting communist totalitarianism, were repressed, deprived of their jobs or forced to emigrate. I ask the Sejm to adopt this resolution by acclamation. [[Applause]] Thank you very much. I must therefore say a few sentences about this drought, even though it seems that the questions did not concern drought. Ladies and gentlemen, in Poland for many years there has been a drought monitoring system run by the Institute of Cultivation, Fertilization and Soil Science in Puławy, which, on the basis of decades of measurements from about 670 metrology stations, determines the climatic water balance, i.e. in some simplification, the ratio of precipitation to evapotranspiration, and thus, even more simply, to evaporate. On the basis of this respect, it is determined in which areas of Poland drought phenomena can occur, which can affect plants. In order to assess the losses, because the State is helping farmers and this year it will also help with a large amount, as is appropriate, and none of the farmers will be harmed and left without aid, so in order to determine what these losses are, an assessment must be carried out in the field. Normally, for many years it has been a system, which is generally a fail-safe system - I would like to say - and from next year I will try to introduce a satellite imaging system which will clearly objectively determine the situation on plantations, what is the state of individual crops. On the other hand, we benefit from the system of committees established by the voivodships on request and in cooperation with the municipalities, which has been developed for several years. This year immediately after droughts began to appear, after warm and humid May and June, such committees began to arise in the municipalities. Luckily, the uncles realized that this was a job for local communities, that setting up these committees was a duty to farmers, not their goodwill. That's why these committees have been coming for weeks. A thousand eight hundred and ninety committees worked in Poland yesterday. Estimates for yesterday - not yet closed, because these committees are still working and in some regions of Poland this work will last a few more days - they say that the losses relate to about 1,700 thousand. Ha, they're touching 91.92,000. holdings. These numbers are probably going to increase a little bit, because it's natural. This is what some Members are so worried about about, and what farmers expect in the form of an entire instrument prepared and functioning for many, many years. We proceed with the consideration of item 44 of the agenda: Information on the status of respect for human and civil rights in 2017 and the activities of the Ombudsman (print No 2602) together with the position of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights (print No 2774). I'm asking you to sit down. I will ask the Ombudsman, Mr Adam Bodnar, who will present the information. [[Some Members get up, give a round of applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-20-sejm-67-3.u527	Dear Marshal! Dear Members, On the basis of the article. 212 of the Constitution I sent to the Sejm on 30 May a comprehensive information on the activities of the Ombudsman and on the status of respect for rights and freedoms in the Republic of Poland in 2017. Today, due to the limited speaking time, I will only allow myself to discuss selected issues. First of all, I would like to stress that the Ombudsman is the most common recipient of citizens' complaints in the country. The number of new complaints has generally remained unchanged for years and remains at more than 20,000. These are systemic issues that have existed for years, but also new issues related to current problems. We also draw our knowledge of human problems directly from letters and matters that are reported by citizens, but also from meetings and conversations with specific people during the office of the spokesperson for regional trips. Since the beginning of my term of office, I have visited more than 140 towns where I organized meetings with residents. We also receive a lot of questions and requests for intervention from Members and Senators. In the current systemic realities, the role of a notary of human harm and a lawyer of citizens' rights is becoming increasingly complex because it is a matter of constantly resolving the dilemma: how much primacy and how much pragmatism. The question arises as to how to protect the rights of citizens in a country where politicians and politicians are increasingly taking over new areas of public life and the subjectivity of citizens is reduced, while at the same time maintaining a similar number of complaints against public institutions in previous years. My answer is determination to remain faithful to the existing constitution, while at the same time seeking solutions that will best serve to protect citizens' rights. I try to fight for the correction of human harm wherever possible today, but I also create places for debate, as illustrated by the First National Congress of Civil Rights last year. More than 1,500 people participated in 36 panels. Congress ended with numerous recommendations. I would like to stress that the objective of my action both in the area of justice and in other areas has never been and is not to maintain a state that has been, because I do not read citizens' expectations this way. The aim is to find a way to talk, to dialogue about how things should be changed, how to improve the justice system, how to take care of the effectiveness of proceedings, how to fight the length of proceedings, how to bring the courts closer to human needs, because I see all these problems, but at the same time I realise that every reform must mean simultaneously respecting the standards of independence of the judiciary, as well as good, loyal cooperation with the courts of other Member States of the European Union. When preparing the 2017 report, we looked at the Ombudsman's activities, as well as the problems encountered in Poland by the prism of specific provisions of the Constitution. We looked at, referring to every subsequent provision of the Constitution, what works, what doesn't work, where the constitution is respected, what has changed and what has not changed and where there are fundamental problems with respect for rights and freedoms. Therefore, this information mentions both the new problems and those that have existed for years in the past year. I would like to stress that I see with all consequence those actions of the state, which serve people well, support from the material and legal point of view the implementation of their citizens' rights. I am thinking about programmes for families and children, legislative changes to strengthen the position of workers, or even some of the steps on the better collection of child support. As a spokesman, I appreciate this direction of change, but of course I also see various problems that still exist in Poland. Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! One of the most important issues is the issue of legislative changes concerning the constitutional system. The greatest concern is the failure to comply with the constitutional principle of social dialogue and the interaction of authorities, which is manifested inter alia. in the absence of consultation of drafts of a number of important laws. Besides, today's meeting was an example of this because we were discussing a parliamentary project, which was probably not It's just a parliamentary project. In addition, the changes made to the functioning of common courts, the National Judicial Council and the Supreme Court increase the impact of political factors on the functioning of the judiciary and, consequently, weaken the system of protection of human rights, as independent courts guarantee the protection of civil rights and freedoms. [[Applause]] I would also like to stress that the role of the Constitutional Tribunal - the next guarantor of civil rights and freedoms - is extremely limited. [[Applause]] I would like to mention that in Poland there are numerous limitations of our rights and freedoms of a personal and political nature. The best example of this is the freedom of assembly, but also what has happened as a result of the restriction of the freedom of assembly, namely often excessive police response and violation of the right to organise counter-fiscation. The threats to the protection of the right to privacy arising from the existence of a number of rules which give the police and special services broad powers to use operational control and data collection of telecommunications, postal and online data or phone records. Both the extent of the data collected about the unit and the continuous lack of effective judicial control over the process of obtaining and using the data are questionable. A very important issue, which concerns both the sphere of social rights and the sphere of personal and political rights, is respect for the rights of persons with disabilities, because we are still far from fulfilling the requirement of the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, which should consist in ensuring the right to independent living for persons with disabilities, but also the right to a decent life for such persons and their carers. In this context, I would like to point out that the Constitutional Tribunal's judgment on financial benefits to caretakers of adults with disabilities is still pending. [[Applause]] It has been a few years and it is really different whether the person who is taking care receives a care service of PLN 1477 or PLN 520, and so many of these persons are receiving a care service as a result of the failure of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. The effects of post-war expropriations and nationalisation have also not been regulated. A comprehensive reprivatisation law is needed to address the problem in a targeted manner. Nor has the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 2015 been enforced until now, ruling on the partial non-constitutionality of the provision enabling the annulment of an administrative decision taken in gross violation of the law without any time limit. This situation undermines the legal certainty of the participants in legal transactions. We have a lot of problems with the increase in prejudiced crimes or even hatred of people of another race, ethnic origin or nationality and religion. In this case, the Ombudsman's office regularly intervenes. We argue for the introduction of a gender reconciliation procedure for transgender people, which will not force them to sue their parents, because it causes further traumatic experiences. [[Applause]] There is still much to be done about respect for the rights of homosexuals and same-sex couples, but also about the fight against homophobia. A very important part of the Ombudsman's work is the rights of parents in the context of the functioning of the modern family. In the Ombudsman's office, we try to alarm ourselves about problems that are not perceived from the perspective of big cities, because there are places in Poland where transport exclusion is becoming an increasing problem, where the young person who finishes junior high school chooses not the school that suits her talents and interests, but the one that can be reached. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-20-sejm-67-3.u593	Dear Marshal! Dear Messengers, Dear Members, Thank you very much for evaluating the work of the Ombudsman's Office, including the evaluation of the work of my collaborators and collaborators. Thank you for all the kind words and appreciation for our efforts. I would like to stress once again that, under these difficult systemic conditions, we are actually trying to do what we can to uphold civil rights and freedoms and help citizens in their difficult situations, but also to suggest many different general solutions that should be implemented. I would like to start by referring to the comments made by Minister Smolinski. And here, using the word "minister" I would like to reserve one thing. Professor Andrzej Zoll once said that it is better to address the Ombudsman: Mr. Spokesman than: Minister, because the Minister symbolises executive authority and the Ombudsman is a spokesman. I think this is correct, which is why, if I could, I would ask - perhaps for the future - to use the concept of a spokesperson. I would refer to a few comments from Minister Smolinski. One point - important - concerned on the one hand the construction of the report, on the other hand - its volume. As far as the construction is concerned, I would like to stress that even when we were preparing information about the state of compliance with rights and freedoms, we saw this through the prism of our actions, i.e. We did not write about things not related to the Ombudsman's office, but we did it in such a way that, you can say, especially bring citizens closer to the activities and problems that exist in Poland and which need to be solved. On the other hand, the volume of the report reminds me of the scene from Miloš Forman's movie Amadeus. Well, Mozart does his work and then asks Emperor Joseph II how he liked it. The Emperor says too many notes. Everyone's so confounded, and Mozart doesn't know what's going on, why too many notes. The Emperor looks at Salieri, and he nods his head, actually too much, too many notes. So I would like to point out that this is simply an image of our activity, but also an image of respect for civil rights and freedoms. This is what Poland looks like in this respect. There aren't enough pages or too many pages here. We were trying to actually give back what is most important in our business. And, of course, there is a subjective question: some may not like it. As regards Minister Smolinski's comment that the Ombudsman is protecting the rights and freedoms set out not only in the Constitution but also in the laws, I would like to point out that one of these most important laws is for us the law on the implementation of certain European Union laws on equal treatment. Of course, the most important issues concerning equal treatment referred to in the report are included in the article. 32 and Article 32 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 33, which concerns equal treatment, but in addition, we always prepare a separate, very specialised report which is devoted to it. Why are there fewer cases? That's something to think about. I think Mr Święcicki is right about the entry into force and obligation of the Law on Free Legal Assistance. Moreover, I recently had a meeting with the law clinics at universities and it turns out that there are fewer cases. But just because there are a little less cases doesn't mean that these cases are less important. This is not about quantity, but about dealing effectively with these issues, and especially about the new type of cases. E.g. a large category of new cases are cases concerning the Law of 16 December 2016, which reduced the pension rights of persons who worked in structures under the Ministry of Home Affairs. There are a lot of these things, and each of them is absolutely unique. Traffic specs cases, expropriation cases require commitment. So even if there are fewer statistically, it doesn't mean we have less work. Minister Smolinski also spoke about the fact that there are fewer judgments of the European Court of Human Rights, i.e. that respect for human rights in Poland is going in the right direction. I would like to point out that the Court, in order to deal with the influx of cases, has for some time applied the technique of ending cases not at the level of the judgment, but at the level of issuing the so-called. unilateral declarations by the government, where the government admits violations, giving rise to compensation or settlement on the basis of settlements. These unilateral declarations and settlements are very many, and therefore there are fewer judgments, so there are much fewer cases reaching this stage of judgment, but that does not mean that people do not get satisfaction in Strasbourg. What is important in this report - and I am glad that Minister Smolinski pointed this out - is the final part that concerns the case law of the European Court of Human Rights. So we wanted to zoom in on what were the most important things. These are two categories. There is a list of judgments of the Court and a list of cases, ladies and gentlemen, communicated to the Polish government, i.e. cases that are taking place, so they are important enough that the Court considers that the government has to comment on these cases. And these are different things. I suspect that it is difficult for you to recognize that the Kornick-Ziobro case against Poland, brought by the mother of Minister Ziobra, is a matter whether the Rabczewska case against Poland, the Solsk and Rybick case against Poland. Finally, issues concerning the Terespol-Brest border crossing point are very important and there are restrictions on applications for refugee status. But these communication issues are important because they show what's going on, what's going on at the stage where the government has to deal with them. And I think it's worth keeping them in the loop. There were a lot of comments about the fact that the Ombudsman allegedly does not deal with housing matters related to reprivatisation. This structure of the report, i.e. a look at the concrete provisions of the Constitution, is again useful here. There's art. 75, which states that public authorities are pursuing policies to meet citizens' housing needs. And here's a whole range of different information on activities in terms of eviction protection on the pavement, including individual cases, for example. the case of Mrs Stefania from Poznań, in which we managed to help very effectively and in principle to win the lawsuit, the case against the so-called. Cleaner of the tenement house. At this point, I would also like to remind you that it was Professor Lipowicz, my predecessor, who led Parliament to adopt laws on criminal law for cleaning tenements, the rules in the Penal Code and the Construction Law. Speaking of housing, I want to say that we have a lot of problems here. It's about the availability of social housing, the most important problem, the issue of energy poverty, which is that even when you get a social space, it's hard to heat it up. There is also a question that arises in some cities, namely paying rent for the non-contractual use of the premises. In this situation, poor people later have a problem with how to pay and have problems on the billing line with a given city. There are a lot of problems in which we try to help on an ongoing basis, both in general terms and in individual terms. On the other hand, the first cause of all these problems is that we do not have a reprivatization bill. If we had a comprehensive reprivatization bill, these problems would have a much smaller coverage. If we are already talking about the rights of residents - it was Minister Smolinski who raised this issue - I would like to draw the attention of the honourable House to the problem of the holders of housing books. In Poland we have 1 million unrealized housing books PKO BP. To at least get that warranty bonus of 7,00,000. zł, you have to have something to use in this book, which means either you have to have your own place or buy it, or you can use it for renovation. I remember a meeting like this, and I talked to one person who was affected by these housing problems, and she said, "What's the matter, Mr. Spokesperson, that I have an apartment book, like I can't even rent an apartment or sell it?" ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-20-sejm-67-3.u608	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On this item I would like to refer to the situation prevailing in the European Railway Consortium Wagon sp. z o.o. in Ostrów Wielkopolski. It is a company with a long, almost 90-year tradition, operating in the repair and delivery industry of new freight wagons. The company, as I mentioned, has a long tradition and has been operating on the market since 1920. The company has been employing over 9 thousand for years. employees and their products were addressed to companies cooperating in the rail transport industry in Europe, Asia, and thus in many parts of the world. At the moment, of course, he is one of the largest employers in Ostrów Wielkopolski and Kalisz, employing about 500 people. Unfortunately, the company has been struggling with problems for many years. Unfortunately, the previous coalition was not very interested in the situation of this company, which, of course, does not matter much from my point of view, is chaired by one of the leaders, former leaders of the Civic Platform Mr Zbigniew Chlebowski. On the other hand, parliamentary elections had to be won so that the company could receive significant assistance and support from the Deputy Prime Minister, the Minister of Development and Finance of Mr Mateusz Morawiecki and the staff of the Ministry, who participated in negotiations and helped to conclude a judicial settlement between the company and PKP Cargo SA. This event, of course, had a huge impact on the process of sanation - from the Latin: sanatio, and thus healing - the situation in this company. One of the many institutions that helped is, of course, the Industrial Development Agency, which granted a loan of around 20 million, which the company paid back in the amount of 18 million plus nearly 2 million interest. Currently, about PLN 2.5 million remain to be repaid. Unfortunately, there are some temporary problems that complicate the payment of part of the commitment. In view of the above, the company requested the Industrial Development Agency to sign a new settlement and suspend recovery operations. With a concern for the maintenance of nearly 500 jobs, I ask the authorities of the Industrial Development Agency to enter into talks so that a new agreement, a new settlement can possibly be concluded and recovery measures can be stopped. I think this is what workers expect, who are worried about their jobs, about losing them, are worried about the situation of their loved ones if the company falls. I share these concerns. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! One cannot overlook the difficult situation of the company, which implies - and thus entails - a difficult situation not only for the railway industry, but above all in the families of the employees of the company. In order to prevent this, I repeat my request to the President of the Industrial Development Agency to take action to sign a new settlement and to halt recovery operations that paralyze the financial and production activities of the company, which is the EKK Wagon in Ostrów Wielkopolski. This is what I ask on my own behalf, but also on behalf of the Chairman of the Management Board of the Wielkopolska Region of the Southern Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union (Solidarity) Mr Jarosław Wilner and the trade unions representing the employees of this company. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-20-sejm-67-3.u610	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Over the last decade, geopolitical changes have accelerated in the world, which seem to lead to the decomposition of a one-polar, constructivist global order that we have dealt with after the Cold War. In addition, we are witnessing a kind of new cultural revolution, which results from the rapid development of mass, rapid communication methods, based on an unprecedented increase in the potential of IT networks. In these circumstances, we can see that some countries and regions of the world have embarked on a very dynamic development that has not been observed in these areas before. The emergence of new economic and financial centres, areas of dynamic population growth or new centres of strength provides an opportunity for those who better recognize the processes, internal tensions and pursuits of others. Undoubtedly, one such region is Latin America. Countries such as Brazil, Mexico and Argentina are becoming more and more important players, whose influence is beginning to go beyond the region. Also, the great potential of natural resources makes this part of the world an area where much of the attention of global players will be focused. It is also worth mentioning that Latin America, with its great ethnic and historical diversity, has a significant potential for a common cultural trunk, which offers the possibility of dynamic interaction between almost all its inhabitants. In this situation, Poland should ensure that its contacts with Latin American countries are properly developed. This requires continuous deepening of competences and knowledge of the issues of the region, culture, economics and politics. Certainly, the great potential that we can use in this matter is well rooted, and often very well-informed representatives of the Polish people in the countries of South and Central America. I hope that fellow Members of Parliament will also be involved in developing solutions to support our activities in the area of Latin America, and this will also support areas such as science, education, the economy, as well as exchanges and cooperation between civil organisations. Thank you.
